                                                                                                                  S A N DY PA R K S , R E C R E AT I O N , A N D T R A I L S M A S T E R P L A N
CANAL TRAILS                                                                        FIGURE 2.3: SANDY WATERWAYS



Canals are an integral part of Sandy’s natural environment. There are around
18 canals and waterways in Sandy. These include:
  •   East Jordan Canal
  •   Jordan and Salt Lake City Canal
  •   Sandy Irrigation Canal
  •   Galena Canal
  •   Union and East Jordan Ditch
  •   Sandy Ditch
  •   Dry Creek
  •   Middle Fork Dry Creek
  •   South Fork Dry Creek
  •   Jordan River
  •   Little Cottonwood Creek
  •   Various unnamed waterways

Canal trails are trails built on the embankments of historic canals and typically
follow the historic route that horses or mules would take to tow boats moving
through the canals. With canals declining with the use of railroad and then
private vehicles, the canal trails fell by the wayside and developed occurred.
In the 1970s and 80s, a resurgence of restoring canal trails occurred to
provide recreation opportunities to cities and connect users to the history
canals played. The number of canals and the interconnectedness of them
provide great opportunities to connect the city through an alternative mode
of transportation.

Sandy City has implemented some canal trails, most notably the Sandy Canal
Trail and the Sandy portion of the Jordan River Parkway and continues to seek
funding to implement more. As these trails are off-street and multiuse, these
trails provide a great opportunity to recreation in the city.

Future trail connections for Canal trails are focused in the western portion of
the city, connecting along the East Jordan Canal, the Jordan and Salt Lake City
Canal Trail, and connecting Dry Creek to the Jordan River.

S A N DY PA R K S , R E C R E AT I O N , A N D T R A I L S M A S T E R P L A N

• Repair walking paths to comply with ADA standards inside the parks

• Enhance or establish new connections to the Cairns District, City Hall, & The Promenade

S A N DY PA R K S , R E C R E AT I O N , A N D T R A I L S M A S T E R P L A N




                                                                                                    EXISTING FACILITIES
                                                                                                    There are several recreation facilities within the Sandy boundaries. Some of the facilities are
                                                                                                    not owned and managed by the city but provide opportunities for residents to recreate.

                                                                                                    Alta Canyon Sports Center
                                                                                                    Alta Canyon Sports Center, located in the eastern region of the city, was built in 1984. The
                                                                                                    center includes an outdoor pool, weight room, cardio room, racquetball courts, and locker
                                                                                                    rooms. In dire need of repairs, the Center applied for grants and funding to expand or renovate
                                                                                                    multiple times but was denied. In 2023, the City Council moved forward with a new build after
                                                                                                    a feasibility study conducted by AECOM. The Center offers various membership opportunities,
                                                                                                    including yearly memberships, summer-only memberships, monthly passes, and day passes.

                                                                                                    Senior Citizens Center
                                                                                                    The senior center offers free membership to individuals throughout Salt Lake County. The
                                                                                                    center has a fitness center, and provides programs such as self-guided yoga, Enhance Fitness




                                                                                       RECREATION
                                                                                                    classes, Tai Chi, Arthritis exercise, square dancing, table tennis, and other exercise classes
                                                                                                    aimed at providing seniors with opportunities to be active. The senior center does not provide
                                                                                                    any outdoor amenities but is located adjacent to the Sandy Amphitheater and Amphitheater
                                                                                                    Park.

                                                                                                    Dimple Dell Recreation Center
                                                                                                    The Dimple Dell Recreation Center is a County owned-and-managed recreation center, which
                                                                                                    includes a fitness area, cycling studio, indoor track, indoor pool, and climbing wall. Membership
                                                                                                    is more costly than the Alta Canyon Sports Center membership, however members get access
                                                                                                    to all Salt Lake County recreation centers. The recreation center does have outdoor field space
                                                                                                    and a walking trail, but no other amenities, including covered picnic areas, playgrounds, or
                                                                                                    benches available to nearby residents.

                                                                                                    River Oaks Golf Course
                                                                                                    The 18-hole golf course is located along the Jordan River, on the boarder between West Jordan
                                                                                                    and Sandy City, The links-style course also houses teaching facilities, a two-sided driving range,
                                                                                                    clubhouse, and Pro Shop.




                                                                                                    While parks, open space, and trails form the foundation of a recreation system, several additional
                                                                                                    layers of facilities and services contribute to the quality of life in a community. Programming in
                                                                                                    the different parks and the Alta Canyon Sports Center fall into this category.

                                                                                                    EXISTING CONDITIONS
                                                                                                    Sandy City has made great strides in reducing overall water consumption on public and privately
                                                                                                    properties. Changes in landscaping requirements for private development and water-wise plantings
                                                                                                    in public spaces have contributed to Sandy’s successful implementation of the city-wide water
                                                                                                    conservation plan. Additional progress can be made by ensuring that the irrigation systems within
                                                                                                    each of the city’s parks functions as efficiently as possible. The team completed an evaluation
                                                                                                    of Sandy’s current system-wide irrigation to identify opportunities for increased efficiency and
                                                                                                    additional reductions in water use.
                                                                                                    The Sandy Parks Department provided GSBS with irrigation as built drawings for the current
                                                                                                    irrigation systems in existing parks. This information was used as the basis of this analysis along
                                                                                                    with our observations during site visits to the parks. The as built drawings document locations
                                                                                                    of water meters, valves, sprinkler heads, controllers, general piping layout and other irrigation
                                                                                                    components. The lists below shows the level of completeness of these as-built documents.

                                          SANDY CITY 2023 PARKS MASTER PLAN SURVEY
                                                                  TOPLINE REPORT



                                                               METHODOLOGY DETAILS

                                                                        n = 509
                                                   Online interviews fielded Oct. 28 – Nov. 15, 2023
                                                                 Margin of error +- 4.3

For this survey, 509 likely voters in Sandy were sampled from the Utah state voter file. Survey invitations were sent via email and text
message with all interviews being completed online. The data were weighted to reflect the demographics of likely general municipal
election voters in Sandy with regard to age, gender, race, and city council district. Weighted frequencies and unweighted sample sizes are
presented throughout this report.

For more information, please contact Kyrene Gibb or Quin Monson at:

Kyrene Gibb, kyrene@y2analytics.com
Quin Monson, quin@y2analytics.com

Y2 Analytics

Salt Lake City, Utah 84111
801-406-7877

Thank you for responding to our survey about important issues in Sandy. Your time and opinions are greatly valued. Please note that your
participation is voluntary and that all your answers will remain strictly confidential.

The survey only takes about 15 minutes to complete. If you exit the survey before completion, you will be able to resume it at a later time.

To begin the survey, click on the forward arrow button below. During the survey you can use the navigation button on the bottom of the
screen to advance questions. If during the survey you do not see the button, scroll down until you see it.

oe_bikecomment. Do you have any additional comments about your experience riding your bike on streets or roads in Sandy? (n=49)

 Bike lanes have always been a problem in sandy. They don't exist even though I think Tim Dolan said he'd provide bike lanes. They are
 just large shoulders on the roads. To late now but it would have been really cool if sandy had been developed like other cities and
 walking/biking trails had to be developed for every mile of streets that were developed.
 Dog walkers are a challenge
 Don’t insist on right of way !
 Drivers are just inattentive. I'm vary cautious and strongly prefer bike paths (multipurpose trails) where you won't have to worry about
 cars except at crossings
 Feels unsafe for kids to bicycle on main roads because of a lack of signage, lighting, and bike lanes.
 Generally bike lanes are great. There are areas where they absolutely are needed and are not present (wasatch) and there are some areas
 where the connections are confusing to drivers where a cyclist is allowed (94th and 13th)
 Get rid of bike lanes that share with traffic - awful for bikers and motorists
 I consider Sandy not very bike friendly. I access porter rockwell and draper parkway trails. They are crowded with multi use and
 interrupted by bumps in the pavement. I like to access the trail to ride out to point of the mountain, but it’s hard to access from Sandy. I
 do not feel that safe on the Jordan parkway trail, so I rarely go there. I feel safe on the open parts, but those are crowded, but then when
 you get down by the river it’s downright isolated and creepy. I would love more biking trails that are not multi use or are wide enough for
 multi use. Too many strollers, dogs, kids, and people walking make the trail un-bike able. Riding on streets is just too risky. Speeding
 cars, driveways…all of it is risky and I try to stick to trails. But there are not enough
 I don’t ride on sidewalks but my kids do and there are lots of streets without sidewalks or missing sections of sidewalk that makes it
 unsafe for my children.
 I have ridden thousands of miles on sand trails and roads. More MTB trails would be wonderful and separting bikes and cars (safer
 roads) would be very nice
 I only ride my bike on Jordan River Trail, due to vehicle unawareness of riders on streets
 It has become unsafe due to distracted drivers
 It's not the lanes that are the problem, it's the drivers :-)
 I’m way more afraid of the drivers than I am the road or biking
 Jordan River Trail or isolate bike lanes feel safe; if I have to ride on roads it feels unsafe. Wish there were more bike lanes.
 More buffered bike lanes would be greatly appreciated. It may increase bike use versus auto use.
 Multiple times cars drift into bike lanes.
 My son was hit by a car in Sandy while on his bike. It has left us rather wary.
 My wife refuses to ride when sharing the road with cars
 Need bike lanes on dimple dell road. Connect to 106th west to bike trail by trax with a bike lane.
 Need to educate drivers about 3 feet rule
